Meciah · Training program · Block 1 · companion to the v2 base document · full-detail edition

Training Block One

Weeks 1–12 of the bedroom program, every session written out in full. The five-day week, the exercises, the progression rules, the skill ladders and the measurements are exactly as the base document settled them; this page adds the calendar, and now says on every line how each thing is actually done, where it starts, and how it progresses. It also carries a full-body flexibility baseline, a strength baseline that never touches a one-rep max, and an honest statement of what should plausibly have moved by week 12.

01What this adds, and what it leaves alone

The base document (Bedroom Training Program v2) is the authority on what you do: the three goals in priority order, the 13 baseline measurements, the Monday Push A / Tuesday Lower + Pull A / Wednesday Skill / Thursday Lower + Pull B / Friday Push B week, double progression, the tier rule, the deload rule, the sleep rule, the 48-hour joint-health override, the skill ladders, and the Reeves ratios as a de-emphasised reference point. Nothing on this page re-opens any of that. Where this page and the base disagree, the base is right and this page has a bug.

What the base deliberately does not say is when. This block puts the rules on a calendar: twelve named weeks, one scheduled deload, one scheduled retest, and an honest statement of how far each ladder should have moved by the end.

What changed in this version, and why

The previous version of this page compressed real instructions into shorthand. "Splits, 10–12 min" told you how long, not how. You said so directly: "When it says something like 'splits, 10 to 12 minutes,' that doesn't really tell me how we're doing it or how we're getting there." You also pointed out that the flexibility work covered the two splits and nothing else, and asked what a non-1RM strength baseline should actually be and what a reasonable expectation of progress looks like.

So Section 03 is now written out in full: every line of every session of every week says what is set up, what a rep or hold is, where the set stops, and how the line progresses. The underlying training logic is unchanged; only the shorthand is gone. Section 07's splits procedure is rewritten the same way, with the dosing evidence and an honest statement of what is and is not proven about PAILs/RAILs. Section 08 is new: a full-body flexibility baseline you can run at home, plus a one-time hypermobility screen. Section 09 is new: the strength baseline without a 1RM. Section 04 now carries week-6 and week-12 expectations for all six ladders and for the flexibility tests.

Why a 12-week block when the week never changes

  • The deload rule only works if it is on the calendar. Week 7 is the deload whether or not week 6 felt hard.
  • Twelve weeks is three monthly tape measurements. Weeks 4, 8 and 12 are the base document's monthly re-measure days.
  • Skill timelines need checkpoints, not just end dates. Section 04 translates each ladder into "where you should plausibly be at week 6 and week 12," so a stall is visible early and a fast run is not mistaken for permission to skip tiers.
  • The research has programming consequences. The FRC procedure, the stretching-dose literature, the Nippard warm-up and the flexibility-mortality cohort each change a specific line of the daily layer or the splits work, on a schedule inside this block.
Still the override

Any new ache that lingers past 48 hours drops that movement back one tier, or out of the session entirely, for that week. Overrides double progression, overrides the tier rule, overrides the deload calendar, overrides every expectation in Section 04 and every effort ceiling in Section 07. No exceptions. If the retest week lands on a sore wrist, the wrist tests are skipped and logged as skipped.

02Shape of the block

Six weeks of loading, one deload, four more weeks of loading, one retest week. The deload at week 7 is the base document's "every 6–8 weeks" rule at its earliest point, chosen because this is a returning lifter's first block and connective tissue is the thing being protected. The retest week is itself run at reduced volume, so the span from week 7 to the next full deload (Block 2, week 7) contains a light week in its middle.

Loading A3–6Double progression running. Expect the first load increases on the big lifts in weeks 3–5.Climb by the tier rule. No testing a new tier in a short-sleep week.Week 4: PAILs/RAILs enter on Wednesday only, at low effort (Section 07).
Deload7Half the sets, same loads, same exercises.Hold the current tier, fewer sets, nothing tested.Gentle daily touch only (Section 07, procedure 2). No intense sessions, no PAILs/RAILs.
Loading B8–11Resume at week-6 loads. Double progression continues.Climb by the tier rule. This is where a second HSPU tier is plausible.PAILs/RAILs on Wednesday and Friday at moderate effort. Monday stays PNF.
Retest12Roughly 60 % of normal sets; one top set per main lift for the log (Section 05).Best clean hold or rep count per ladder, fresh.Gentle daily touch; the measurement is support height, not a max stretch.

Why the deload is half-volume and not a week off

The only randomised trial that has tested a one-week deload directly (Coleman et al., 2024, PeerJ) used a complete week off in week 5 of a 9-week programme in resistance-trained young adults (about 21–22 years old, three-plus years of training). Muscle growth was the same whether the week off was taken or not; lower-body strength gained a little more in the group that trained straight through. The authors themselves noted that coaches almost never deload by stopping completely, and that reduced-volume deloads were not tested. Two conclusions follow for this block. First, a deload will not cost you muscle, so there is no reason to skip it. Second, the half-volume version the base document prescribes keeps you lifting, which is the version least likely to show the small strength dip, and the one most compatible with the point of the deload here: connective tissue, which those trials did not measure at all.

03Week by week, every session written out

Sixty sessions plus the rest days, each written in full. The five-day structure, the exercises, the sets and rep ranges are exactly the base document's; what this section adds is the mechanism on every line: how the movement is set up, what one rep or one hold consists of, where the set stops, and how the line moves forward. The daily layer (pulse-raiser, CARs, cuff wake-up on press days, gentle split touch on lower and rest days) is written out every day on purpose, because it is the part most likely to be skipped when it is a one-word label.

Three conventions. "1–3 reps shy of failure" means the set ends while the next rep would still look like the one before it. Hand-support height for the splits is a measurement (blocks / fingers / none), never an assumption; whatever height you need today is the correct one. The 48-hour override sits above every line below without being repeated on each one.

How to read a week

Each week opens with what is different about it. The session cards then give every line in full. Where a line's text is identical to the previous week, that is deliberate: the structure repeats and only the loads, reps, holds, tiers and support heights move.

Week 1

Nov 23 – 27
placement

Placement week. Every weight is found, not assumed; every ladder is placed by one fresh test of its tier-1 criterion; every baseline number (splits support height, longevity battery, full-body flexibility battery, Beighton) is written down once. From today every session opens with the pulse-raiser then CARs.

Monday · Push A ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Bench press (floor press until stands arrive). 4 × 6–12 Floor press: lie on the stall mat, roll the loaded bar over your chest, elbows and upper arms resting on the floor at the bottom. Grip just outside shoulder width, wrists stacked over elbows, shoulder blades pulled down and together, feet flat. Press until the elbows lock, lower under control until the upper arms touch the floor (about two seconds down), pause a beat, press. Every rep from a dead stop on the floor. Stop the set at 1–3 reps shy of failure: the last rep looks like the first, no bouncing the arms off the floor. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Overhead press, cleaned from the floor. 3 × 6–12 Bar on the floor, grip just outside the shoulders, pull it to the collarbone in one movement (the clean). Then: squeeze glutes, brace the trunk, tuck the chin back out of the bar path, press straight up until the elbows lock with the bar over the back of the head and the ears in line with the arms. Lower to the collarbone in two seconds. No leg drive, no lean-back. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; the rep where the low back starts arching is the rep you did not do. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Ring dips (bench dips if rings are not yet clean for 3). 3 × 8–12 Rings at hip height, straps tight. Jump to a support hold: arms locked, rings turned slightly out, shoulders pressed down away from the ears. Lower by bending the elbows, keeping them pointing back, until the shoulder is level with or just below the elbow, two seconds down, press back to lockout. Stop the set the moment the rings start wobbling outward or the shoulders roll forward at the bottom. Rest 2 minutes. Bench dips: hands on the bench edge behind you, feet on the floor, same depth and tempo. Progression on rings is reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Planche-lean primer. 3 × 15–25 s Kneel, place the hands on the floor shoulder-width with the fingers pointing out to the sides or back toward the knees, arms locked. Lift the knees and shift the shoulders forward past the wrists, as far as you can while the elbows stay locked and the ribs stay pulled in (hollow body, no belly hanging). Feet stay on the floor. Hold 15–25 seconds. End the hold while the elbows are still locked and the hips still high: the moment either starts to go, the hold is over. Rest 90 seconds. Progression: hold time 15 → 25 s, then shoulders further forward; this is a primer for Wednesday's ladder, not a hard set. This week: placement only on the Wednesday ladders; here, work the tier you are confident in and log hold time or reps.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15–20 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 3 × 12–20 Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Intense split work: static holds + PNF contract-relax, 10–12 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. On this shorter session run 2 cycles per position; a third only if the first two felt easy. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.
  • Log, before leaving the mat. Hand-support height for front split left, front split right (blocks / fingers / none) and side split hip height in hand-widths. This is the baseline number that tracks the splits all block.

Tuesday · Lower + Pull A ≈ 55–60 min knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase. The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Goblet squat warm-up. 2 × 8, light Dumbbell held vertically against the chest, feet shoulder-width, toes slightly out. Sit between the heels to full depth with the heels down and the elbows inside the knees, stand. Warm-up only; the pistol ladder replaces this from week 2.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches. Done inside the pistol rest periods.
  • Squat (Zercher or front squat without a rack; Bulgarian split squat until the bar arrives). 4 × 6–12 Zercher: bar on the bench, squat down, hook it in the crooks of the elbows, hands clasped, stand. Feet shoulder-width, toes slightly out. Sit between the heels with the chest up until the thighs are at least parallel, heels down, knees tracking over the toes, then drive up. Two seconds down, one up. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure, or at the rep where the heels lift or the knees cave. Rest 2–3 minutes. If still on split squats: rear foot on the bench, dumbbells in hand, same depth and tempo, 4 × 6–12 per leg. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Romanian deadlift. 3 × 8–12 Bar held at the hips, feet hip-width, knees softly bent and staying that way. Push the hips straight back and let the bar slide down the thighs, back flat, until the hamstrings are fully stretched (usually mid-shin), then drive the hips forward to stand. Three seconds down, one up. The bar never touches the floor between reps. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or when the back begins to round. Rest 2 minutes. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Pull-ups, overhand. 3 × 6–12 Dead hang from the doorway bar, hands just outside shoulder-width, palms away. Pull the shoulder blades down first, then the elbows to the ribs, until the chin is clearly over the bar. Lower fully to a straight-arm hang, two seconds. No kip, no swing. Stop at the first rep that does not clear the bar cleanly. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: reps 6 → 12, then a plate in a backpack and back to 6. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Barbell row (ring row, feet elevated, until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Bar on the floor, hinge to about 45°, grip just outside the knees. Pull the bar to the lower ribs, elbows passing the torso, shoulder blades squeezing, pause a beat, lower in two seconds. The torso angle does not change; if it rises to lift the bar, the set is over. Ring row: lie back under the rings with heels on the bench, body a straight line, pull the chest to the rings, lower slowly. Rest 2 minutes. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Ball of one foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, other hand on the wall for balance only. Lower the heel to a full stretch, pause one second, rise as high as possible, squeeze one second. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Progression: reps 10 → 20, then heavier dumbbell and back to 10. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.

Wednesday · Skill ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist · spine (extension)

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs with extra wrist rotations, 6–8 min. Same procedure as every day (one joint at a time, 20–30 % whole-body tension, 15–30 seconds per rotation, neck → shoulders → elbows → wrists → spine → hips → knees → ankles), but the wrists get 5–6 rotations per direction instead of 3, plus a set of slow finger-closed to finger-open circles, because today loads the wrist in extension more than any other day.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Wrist and shoulder prep: wrist push-ups 2 × 10 (fingers forward, then backward), scapular push-ups 2 × 10, hollow-body hold 2 × 20–30 s. Wrist push-ups: on all fours, hands flat, rock the weight forward over the hands until the wrists are at the edge of comfortable extension, then push the palms down so the heels of the hands rise; 10 slow reps, then turn the fingers to point back toward the knees and repeat. Scapular push-ups: plank on locked arms, let the shoulder blades pinch together so the chest drops an inch, then push the floor away until the upper back rounds; elbows never bend. Hollow hold: lie on your back, low back pressed into the mat, arms overhead, legs straight and lifted a few inches, ribs pulled down; hold 20–30 seconds breathing.
  • Planche ladder placement test. 1 attempt at tier 1, fresh, then 4 practice sets Set up the planche lean exactly as described (hands shoulder-width, fingers out or back, elbows locked, shoulders forward past the wrists, hollow body, feet down) and hold once for as long as it stays clean. A clean 30 seconds with the shoulders well past the wrists means tier 2 (tuck) is the working tier from next week; anything less means tier 1 is. Do not attempt the tuck today. Then 4 lean practice sets of 15–25 s, 2–3 minutes' rest.
  • Handstand push-up ladder placement test. 1 set of tier 1, fresh, then 3 practice sets Pike push-up: hips high, head to the floor between the hands, press to lockout. One set to the first non-clean rep. 10 clean reps means tier 2 (feet on the bench) from next week; fewer means tier 1. Do not test tier 2 today. Then 3 practice sets at the tier you landed on, stopped well short of failure, 2–3 minutes' rest.
  • Intense split work: static holds + PNF contract-relax, 12–20 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. This is the main weekly session, so run the full 2–4 contract-relax cycles on all three positions; if time is short, the side split keeps its cycles and the front split drops to 2 each. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.
  • Log, before leaving the mat. Hand-support height for both front splits and side split hip height, same as Monday; if different from Monday, record both and use the better one as baseline.
  • Longevity battery, once, five minutes (Section 05). 4 tests Sitting-rising test: stand barefoot, lower to sitting cross-legged and stand back up with as little support as possible; 5 points down, 5 up, minus 1 per hand/forearm/knee/side-of-leg support, minus 0.5 per wobble. One-leg stance: eyes open, arms at sides, free foot against the standing calf, 10 s target each side, up to 3 tries. Standing toe reach: feet together, knees straight, reach down slowly, fingertip-to-floor in cm (negative if past the floor, measured on a block). Knee-to-wall: half-kneeling facing a wall, test foot flat, drive the knee to the wall without the heel lifting, slide the foot back until the knee just touches, measure big toe to wall in cm each side.
  • Full-body flexibility battery, once, about ten minutes (Section 08). 4 tests + one-time Beighton screen Apley's scratch (both sides), modified Thomas test (both legs), seated trunk rotation (both directions), sit-and-reach; each exactly as Section 08 describes, measured with the tape and written down. Then the nine-point Beighton screen, once, not tracked: it tells you whether you are tight, average or unusually loose, which decides how hard PAILs/RAILs are ever pushed.

Thursday · Lower + Pull B ≈ 55–60 min knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase. The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Deadlift, from the floor. 3 × 6–10 Bar over the middle of the feet, feet hip-width. Hinge down, grip just outside the shins, shins touching the bar, chest up, back flat, arms straight. Push the floor away: hips and shoulders rise together, bar dragging up the shins and thighs until you are standing tall. Lower by pushing the hips back and bending the knees once the bar passes them, two seconds down, bar touching the floor and resetting between reps (no bouncing). Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or at the first rep where the lower back rounds. Rest 3 minutes. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Bulgarian split squat. 4 × 8–12 per leg Rear foot laced on the bench, front foot far enough forward that the front shin stays roughly vertical at the bottom, dumbbells or plates hanging at the sides. Lower straight down until the rear knee nearly touches the floor and the front thigh is at least parallel, two seconds, drive up through the front heel. Front knee tracks over the middle toes. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Rest 90 seconds between legs or 2 minutes between pairs. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then add load. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Chin-ups, underhand. 3 × 6–12 Dead hang, palms facing you, hands shoulder-width. Shoulder blades down first, then pull until the chin is clearly over the bar, lower fully in two seconds. No kip. Stop at the first rep that does not clear. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: 6 → 12 reps, then backpack load, back to 6. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Barbell row or chest-supported row on the bench (ring row until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Chest-supported: lie face-down on the bench set flat or on a slight incline, dumbbells or bar hanging below, pull to the lower ribs with the elbows passing the torso, squeeze the shoulder blades one second, lower in two seconds. Barbell row as Tuesday. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; momentum or a shifting torso ends the set. Rest 2 minutes. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Exactly as Tuesday: ball of the foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, full stretch at the bottom with a one-second pause, full rise with a one-second squeeze, two seconds down. 1–3 reps shy of failure. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Hanging leg raise or knee raise. 3 × 8–15 Dead hang from the pull-up bar, shoulders pulled down. Knee raise: pull the knees up to the chest, curling the pelvis under at the top, lower in two seconds without swinging. Leg raise: legs straight, raise until the feet reach bar height. Stop when the swing starts. Progression: knee raise 8 → 15, then straight-leg at 8.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches.

Friday · Push B ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Close-grip bench press (close-grip floor press until stands arrive). 3 × 8–12 As Monday's floor press but with the hands shoulder-width or a thumb's length inside it, elbows tucked closer to the ribs on the way down so the upper arms touch the floor nearer the body. Dead stop on the floor each rep, two seconds down, press to lockout. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier, as the day's vertical push. 4 × tier reps Same tier and same execution as Wednesday (pike: head to floor between the hands and press to lockout; elevated pike: feet on the bench; wall negatives: three-second lower to head touch; partial press: stacked books set the depth). Four sets, each stopped well short of failure, 2–3 minutes' rest. If Wednesday left any shoulder or wrist tenderness, do pike push-ups one tier down instead; the second weekly exposure is for practice volume, not for testing. This week: placement only on the Wednesday ladders; here, work the tier you are confident in and log hold time or reps.
  • Ring push-ups, feet elevated or plate on the back. 3 × 8–12 Rings a few inches off the floor, straps tight, feet on the bench. Plank on locked arms with the rings turned slightly out, lower until the chest is level with the rings with the elbows pointing back at about 45°, two seconds, press back to lockout and turn the rings out again. Body stays a straight line; the set ends when the hips sag or the rings drift. Rest 2 minutes. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Dumbbell lateral raise. 3 × 12–20 Dumbbells at the sides, slight forward lean, elbows soft. Raise out to the side leading with the elbows until the arms are parallel to the floor, pinkies level with or slightly above the thumbs, one second up, two seconds down. No shrug, no swing from the hips. 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: 12 → 20 reps, then the next dumbbell setting at 12. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15–20 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 3 × 12–20 Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. This week: pick a weight you can lift for the bottom of the range (6 for 6–12, 10 or 12 for the higher ranges) with 3 reps clearly in reserve. If unsure, go lighter; reps get added next week. Barbell lifts use the no-barbell substitution until the bar lands.
  • Intense split work: static holds + PNF contract-relax, 10–12 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. On this shorter session run 2 cycles per position; a third only if the first two felt easy. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.

Saturday and Sunday · rest days ≈ 10–15 min daily layer only

  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.

  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Nothing else. No pulse-raiser (training days only), no lifting, no intense stretching. Sleep 7–9 hours; a week averaging under 7 means no new tier is tested next week.

Week 2

Nov 30 – Dec 4
placement

Second placement week. Weights stay, reps are added. The one-time strength tests (strict max-rep dips, pull-ups, push-ups) and the estimated-max sets for the five barbell lifts replace one working set each, so no session gets longer. The pistol and bridge ladders enter and are placed. Film one set of every main lift from the side. From here the sleep rule applies.

Monday · Push A ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Estimated-max set, no 1RM (Section 09) for the bench/floor press and the overhead press. 1 set, 2–4 reps in reserve Replaces the first working set of this lift this week only. Load a weight heavier than the working weight, one you expect to lift about 5 times. Perform the set with the same technique standard as every set, stopping with 2–4 reps clearly left (3–8 reps achieved is the useful range). Record weight × reps and compute 1RM estimate = weight × (36 ÷ (37 − reps)). The estimate is written down; it is never attempted. Remaining sets at the normal working weight.
  • Bench press (floor press until stands arrive). 4 × 6–12 Floor press: lie on the stall mat, roll the loaded bar over your chest, elbows and upper arms resting on the floor at the bottom. Grip just outside shoulder width, wrists stacked over elbows, shoulder blades pulled down and together, feet flat. Press until the elbows lock, lower under control until the upper arms touch the floor (about two seconds down), pause a beat, press. Every rep from a dead stop on the floor. Stop the set at 1–3 reps shy of failure: the last rep looks like the first, no bouncing the arms off the floor. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Overhead press, cleaned from the floor. 3 × 6–12 Bar on the floor, grip just outside the shoulders, pull it to the collarbone in one movement (the clean). Then: squeeze glutes, brace the trunk, tuck the chin back out of the bar path, press straight up until the elbows lock with the bar over the back of the head and the ears in line with the arms. Lower to the collarbone in two seconds. No leg drive, no lean-back. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; the rep where the low back starts arching is the rep you did not do. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Strict max-rep dip test (one time, replaces the first dip set). 1 set to the first non-clean rep Rings (or bars if you have them): support hold, lower until the shoulder is level with or below the elbow, press to full lockout, no swing, steady tempo. Count every clean rep; stop at the first rep that is shallow, swings, or does not lock out. Log reps and 'ring' or 'bar'. If three clean ring dips are not yet there, log 'not yet testable'; bench dips do not count. Then the remaining 2 dip sets as normal.
  • Planche-lean primer. 3 × 15–25 s Kneel, place the hands on the floor shoulder-width with the fingers pointing out to the sides or back toward the knees, arms locked. Lift the knees and shift the shoulders forward past the wrists, as far as you can while the elbows stay locked and the ribs stay pulled in (hollow body, no belly hanging). Feet stay on the floor. Hold 15–25 seconds. End the hold while the elbows are still locked and the hips still high: the moment either starts to go, the hold is over. Rest 90 seconds. Progression: hold time 15 → 25 s, then shoulders further forward; this is a primer for Wednesday's ladder, not a hard set. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15–20 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 3 × 12–20 Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Intense split work: static holds + PNF contract-relax, 10–12 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. On this shorter session run 2 cycles per position; a third only if the first two felt easy. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.
  • Film. One set of the floor press and one of the overhead press from the side, phone propped on the bench. This is the week-12 comparison footage.

Tuesday · Lower + Pull A ≈ 55–60 min knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Pistol squat ladder, current tier (as the squat's warm-up). 3 sets per leg, alternating, 90 s rest per pair Done straight after the daily layer, in place of the goblet warm-up. Tier 1, bench pistol: stand on one leg in front of the flat bench (about 45 cm), other leg held straight out front, arms reached forward, shoulders rounded forward. Sit back to the bench under control with the heel down and the knee tracking over the middle of the foot, touch, stand without the free foot touching down. Tier 2 is the same to a 25–30 cm target. Tier 3 is full depth (hamstring on calf) with a fingertip on the doorframe or a 5–10 lb plate held out front as a counterweight. Tier 4 is unassisted. Stop every set at the first rep where the heel lifts or the knee dives inward. Pass criteria: tiers 1–2, 3 × 8 clean per leg; tiers 3–4, 3 × 5. Ankle gate first: knee-to-wall under 10 cm keeps the pistol at tier 1 regardless of strength. Wrist prehab sets (below) are done inside these rest periods. Placement this week: first read the week-1 knee-to-wall result (ankle gate: 10 cm or more each side, plus a heels-down full two-leg squat). Then test tier 1, the bench pistol, once, fresh: 3 × 8 per leg clean passes you to tier 2 next week; anything less and tier 1 is the working tier.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches. Done inside the pistol rest periods.
  • Estimated-max set, no 1RM (Section 09) for the squat variant and the Romanian deadlift. 1 set, 2–4 reps in reserve Replaces the first working set of this lift this week only. Load a weight heavier than the working weight, one you expect to lift about 5 times. Perform the set with the same technique standard as every set, stopping with 2–4 reps clearly left (3–8 reps achieved is the useful range). Record weight × reps and compute 1RM estimate = weight × (36 ÷ (37 − reps)). The estimate is written down; it is never attempted. Remaining sets at the normal working weight.
  • Squat (Zercher or front squat without a rack; Bulgarian split squat until the bar arrives). 4 × 6–12 Zercher: bar on the bench, squat down, hook it in the crooks of the elbows, hands clasped, stand. Feet shoulder-width, toes slightly out. Sit between the heels with the chest up until the thighs are at least parallel, heels down, knees tracking over the toes, then drive up. Two seconds down, one up. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure, or at the rep where the heels lift or the knees cave. Rest 2–3 minutes. If still on split squats: rear foot on the bench, dumbbells in hand, same depth and tempo, 4 × 6–12 per leg. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Romanian deadlift. 3 × 8–12 Bar held at the hips, feet hip-width, knees softly bent and staying that way. Push the hips straight back and let the bar slide down the thighs, back flat, until the hamstrings are fully stretched (usually mid-shin), then drive the hips forward to stand. Three seconds down, one up. The bar never touches the floor between reps. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or when the back begins to round. Rest 2 minutes. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Strict max-rep pull-up test (one time, replaces the first pull-up set). 1 set to the first non-clean rep Dead hang, overhand, hands just outside shoulder-width. Pull until the chin is clearly over the bar, lower to a full straight-arm hang each rep, no kip, no swing. Stop at the first rep that does not clear. Log reps and place the number on the scale: 5 = Beginner I, 12 = Intermediate I, 20 = Advanced I. Then the remaining 2 pull-up sets as normal.
  • Barbell row (ring row, feet elevated, until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Bar on the floor, hinge to about 45°, grip just outside the knees. Pull the bar to the lower ribs, elbows passing the torso, shoulder blades squeezing, pause a beat, lower in two seconds. The torso angle does not change; if it rises to lift the bar, the set is over. Ring row: lie back under the rings with heels on the bench, body a straight line, pull the chest to the rings, lower slowly. Rest 2 minutes. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Ball of one foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, other hand on the wall for balance only. Lower the heel to a full stretch, pause one second, rise as high as possible, squeeze one second. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Progression: reps 10 → 20, then heavier dumbbell and back to 10. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Film. One set of the squat variant and one RDL from the side.

Wednesday · Skill ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist · spine (extension)

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs with extra wrist rotations, 6–8 min. Same procedure as every day (one joint at a time, 20–30 % whole-body tension, 15–30 seconds per rotation, neck → shoulders → elbows → wrists → spine → hips → knees → ankles), but the wrists get 5–6 rotations per direction instead of 3, plus a set of slow finger-closed to finger-open circles, because today loads the wrist in extension more than any other day.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Wrist and shoulder prep: wrist push-ups 2 × 10 (fingers forward, then backward), scapular push-ups 2 × 10, hollow-body hold 2 × 20–30 s. Wrist push-ups: on all fours, hands flat, rock the weight forward over the hands until the wrists are at the edge of comfortable extension, then push the palms down so the heels of the hands rise; 10 slow reps, then turn the fingers to point back toward the knees and repeat. Scapular push-ups: plank on locked arms, let the shoulder blades pinch together so the chest drops an inch, then push the floor away until the upper back rounds; elbows never bend. Hollow hold: lie on your back, low back pressed into the mat, arms overhead, legs straight and lifted a few inches, ribs pulled down; hold 20–30 seconds breathing.
  • Planche ladder, current tier. 5–6 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, planche lean: hands on the floor, fingers out or back, arms locked, shoulders pushed forward past the wrists, body hollow, feet on the floor; hold stopped while the elbows are still locked and hips still high. Pass: 3–4 sets of 30 s clean with the shoulders well past the wrists. Tier 2, tuck planche: from the lean, knees tucked to the chest, feet lifted off the floor, hips level with the shoulders. Pass: 30 s clean. Tier 3 advanced tuck (back flat, hips opened to about 90°), tier 4 straddle (legs straight and wide, pass 15–20 s), tier 5 full. Every set ends at the first sign of form loss: hips dropping, elbows bending, shoulders drifting back. 5–6 sets with 2–3 minutes' rest; this is practice, not a workout. Fall short of a new tier two sessions running: back one tier for a week, retry.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier. 4–5 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, pike push-up: hands and feet on the floor, hips pushed high so the torso points at the floor, lower the top of the head to the floor between the hands, press back to locked arms. Pass: 3–4 sets of 10 clean. Tier 2, elevated pike: feet on the bench, torso near vertical, same movement. Pass: 3–4 × 10. Tier 3, wall negatives: kick up to a wall handstand, lower over three full seconds until the head touches, kick down, reset. Pass: 5 × 3 with a 3-second lower. Tier 4, partial-range wall press: stacked books or a block under the head set the depth; press out of the bottom quarter, then half, then three-quarters, 3 × 5 at each depth before shrinking the stack. Tier 5: 3 or more strict full wall HSPU. Rest 2–3 minutes between sets, every set stopped well short of failure. Fall short two sessions running on a new tier: back one tier for a week.
  • Intense split work: static holds + PNF contract-relax, 12–20 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. This is the main weekly session, so run the full 2–4 contract-relax cycles on all three positions; if time is short, the side split keeps its cycles and the front split drops to 2 each. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.
  • Back bridge ladder, current tier (last, inside the mobility block). 2–3 sets, 60–90 s rest Tier 1 glute bridge: on your back, knees bent, feet flat, press the hips up until knees-hips-shoulders are a straight line, 2-second squeeze; pass 3 × 12. Tier 2 straight bridge (reverse plank): seated, legs straight, hands on the floor behind, press the hips up until heels-to-shoulders is one line, head neutral, 2-second hold; pass 3 × 12. Tier 3 wall bridge: back to a wall a step away, reach overhead and back, hands on the wall, walk them down a few hand-widths toward hip height and back up, eyes on the hands throughout; pass 3 × 8. Tier 4 incline bridge: hands flat on the bench, fingers pointing toward the feet, feet on the floor, press up into a bridge with hips high and arms as straight as they go, eyes on the hands, 3-second hold; pass 3 × 8. Tier 5 full bridge: hands and feet on the floor, hips high, head off the floor, elbows may bend, 5-second hold; pass 3 × 5. Tier 6 wheel-quality: arms straight, shoulders over the wrists, feet walked in; 3 × 15–20 s. Exhale into every position, four counts in and four out. A pinch at the base of the spine, anything in the neck, or pain at the front of the shoulder or in the wrist ends the set and starts the 48-hour clock. If bench-to-floor is too big a jump, put the hands on a stack of plates and lower it a few centimetres at a time. Placement this week: one set each of tiers 1 and 2 (they will almost certainly pass), then one attempt at tier 3, the wall bridge. Most likely start is tier 3 or 4; log it.

Thursday · Lower + Pull B ≈ 55–60 min knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase. The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Estimated-max set, no 1RM (Section 09) for the deadlift. 1 set, 2–4 reps in reserve Replaces the first working set of this lift this week only. Load a weight heavier than the working weight, one you expect to lift about 5 times. Perform the set with the same technique standard as every set, stopping with 2–4 reps clearly left (3–8 reps achieved is the useful range). Record weight × reps and compute 1RM estimate = weight × (36 ÷ (37 − reps)). The estimate is written down; it is never attempted. Remaining sets at the normal working weight.
  • Deadlift, from the floor. 3 × 6–10 Bar over the middle of the feet, feet hip-width. Hinge down, grip just outside the shins, shins touching the bar, chest up, back flat, arms straight. Push the floor away: hips and shoulders rise together, bar dragging up the shins and thighs until you are standing tall. Lower by pushing the hips back and bending the knees once the bar passes them, two seconds down, bar touching the floor and resetting between reps (no bouncing). Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or at the first rep where the lower back rounds. Rest 3 minutes. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Bulgarian split squat. 4 × 8–12 per leg Rear foot laced on the bench, front foot far enough forward that the front shin stays roughly vertical at the bottom, dumbbells or plates hanging at the sides. Lower straight down until the rear knee nearly touches the floor and the front thigh is at least parallel, two seconds, drive up through the front heel. Front knee tracks over the middle toes. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Rest 90 seconds between legs or 2 minutes between pairs. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then add load. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Chin-ups, underhand. 3 × 6–12 Dead hang, palms facing you, hands shoulder-width. Shoulder blades down first, then pull until the chin is clearly over the bar, lower fully in two seconds. No kip. Stop at the first rep that does not clear. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: 6 → 12 reps, then backpack load, back to 6. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Barbell row or chest-supported row on the bench (ring row until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Chest-supported: lie face-down on the bench set flat or on a slight incline, dumbbells or bar hanging below, pull to the lower ribs with the elbows passing the torso, squeeze the shoulder blades one second, lower in two seconds. Barbell row as Tuesday. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; momentum or a shifting torso ends the set. Rest 2 minutes. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Exactly as Tuesday: ball of the foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, full stretch at the bottom with a one-second pause, full rise with a one-second squeeze, two seconds down. 1–3 reps shy of failure. This week: same weight as week 1, add reps to every set. Only if a set reaches the top of the range with reps still in reserve may this lift take the smallest increment (2.5 lb per side, one backpack plate, next dumbbell setting) and restart at the bottom.
  • Hanging leg raise or knee raise. 3 × 8–15 Dead hang from the pull-up bar, shoulders pulled down. Knee raise: pull the knees up to the chest, curling the pelvis under at the top, lower in two seconds without swinging. Leg raise: legs straight, raise until the feet reach bar height. Stop when the swing starts. Progression: knee raise 8 → 15, then straight-leg at 8.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches.
  • Film. One deadlift set and one split-squat set from the side.

Friday · Push B ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist
