For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. On this shorter session run 2 cycles per position; a third only if the first two felt easy. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.

Tuesday · Lower + Pull A ≈ 55–60 min knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase. The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Pistol squat ladder, current tier (as the squat's warm-up). 3 sets per leg, alternating, 90 s rest per pair Done straight after the daily layer, in place of the goblet warm-up. Tier 1, bench pistol: stand on one leg in front of the flat bench (about 45 cm), other leg held straight out front, arms reached forward, shoulders rounded forward. Sit back to the bench under control with the heel down and the knee tracking over the middle of the foot, touch, stand without the free foot touching down. Tier 2 is the same to a 25–30 cm target. Tier 3 is full depth (hamstring on calf) with a fingertip on the doorframe or a 5–10 lb plate held out front as a counterweight. Tier 4 is unassisted. Stop every set at the first rep where the heel lifts or the knee dives inward. Pass criteria: tiers 1–2, 3 × 8 clean per leg; tiers 3–4, 3 × 5. Ankle gate first: knee-to-wall under 10 cm keeps the pistol at tier 1 regardless of strength. Wrist prehab sets (below) are done inside these rest periods. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches. Done inside the pistol rest periods.
  • Squat (Zercher or front squat without a rack; Bulgarian split squat until the bar arrives). 4 × 6–12 Zercher: bar on the bench, squat down, hook it in the crooks of the elbows, hands clasped, stand. Feet shoulder-width, toes slightly out. Sit between the heels with the chest up until the thighs are at least parallel, heels down, knees tracking over the toes, then drive up. Two seconds down, one up. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure, or at the rep where the heels lift or the knees cave. Rest 2–3 minutes. If still on split squats: rear foot on the bench, dumbbells in hand, same depth and tempo, 4 × 6–12 per leg. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Romanian deadlift. 3 × 8–12 Bar held at the hips, feet hip-width, knees softly bent and staying that way. Push the hips straight back and let the bar slide down the thighs, back flat, until the hamstrings are fully stretched (usually mid-shin), then drive the hips forward to stand. Three seconds down, one up. The bar never touches the floor between reps. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or when the back begins to round. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Pull-ups, overhand. 3 × 6–12 Dead hang from the doorway bar, hands just outside shoulder-width, palms away. Pull the shoulder blades down first, then the elbows to the ribs, until the chin is clearly over the bar. Lower fully to a straight-arm hang, two seconds. No kip, no swing. Stop at the first rep that does not clear the bar cleanly. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: reps 6 → 12, then a plate in a backpack and back to 6. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Barbell row (ring row, feet elevated, until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Bar on the floor, hinge to about 45°, grip just outside the knees. Pull the bar to the lower ribs, elbows passing the torso, shoulder blades squeezing, pause a beat, lower in two seconds. The torso angle does not change; if it rises to lift the bar, the set is over. Ring row: lie back under the rings with heels on the bench, body a straight line, pull the chest to the rings, lower slowly. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Ball of one foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, other hand on the wall for balance only. Lower the heel to a full stretch, pause one second, rise as high as possible, squeeze one second. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Progression: reps 10 → 20, then heavier dumbbell and back to 10. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.

Wednesday · Skill ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist · spine (extension)

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs with extra wrist rotations, 6–8 min. Same procedure as every day (one joint at a time, 20–30 % whole-body tension, 15–30 seconds per rotation, neck → shoulders → elbows → wrists → spine → hips → knees → ankles), but the wrists get 5–6 rotations per direction instead of 3, plus a set of slow finger-closed to finger-open circles, because today loads the wrist in extension more than any other day.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Wrist and shoulder prep: wrist push-ups 2 × 10 (fingers forward, then backward), scapular push-ups 2 × 10, hollow-body hold 2 × 20–30 s. Wrist push-ups: on all fours, hands flat, rock the weight forward over the hands until the wrists are at the edge of comfortable extension, then push the palms down so the heels of the hands rise; 10 slow reps, then turn the fingers to point back toward the knees and repeat. Scapular push-ups: plank on locked arms, let the shoulder blades pinch together so the chest drops an inch, then push the floor away until the upper back rounds; elbows never bend. Hollow hold: lie on your back, low back pressed into the mat, arms overhead, legs straight and lifted a few inches, ribs pulled down; hold 20–30 seconds breathing.
  • Planche ladder, current tier. 5–6 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, planche lean: hands on the floor, fingers out or back, arms locked, shoulders pushed forward past the wrists, body hollow, feet on the floor; hold stopped while the elbows are still locked and hips still high. Pass: 3–4 sets of 30 s clean with the shoulders well past the wrists. Tier 2, tuck planche: from the lean, knees tucked to the chest, feet lifted off the floor, hips level with the shoulders. Pass: 30 s clean. Tier 3 advanced tuck (back flat, hips opened to about 90°), tier 4 straddle (legs straight and wide, pass 15–20 s), tier 5 full. Every set ends at the first sign of form loss: hips dropping, elbows bending, shoulders drifting back. 5–6 sets with 2–3 minutes' rest; this is practice, not a workout. Fall short of a new tier two sessions running: back one tier for a week, retry. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier. 4–5 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, pike push-up: hands and feet on the floor, hips pushed high so the torso points at the floor, lower the top of the head to the floor between the hands, press back to locked arms. Pass: 3–4 sets of 10 clean. Tier 2, elevated pike: feet on the bench, torso near vertical, same movement. Pass: 3–4 × 10. Tier 3, wall negatives: kick up to a wall handstand, lower over three full seconds until the head touches, kick down, reset. Pass: 5 × 3 with a 3-second lower. Tier 4, partial-range wall press: stacked books or a block under the head set the depth; press out of the bottom quarter, then half, then three-quarters, 3 × 5 at each depth before shrinking the stack. Tier 5: 3 or more strict full wall HSPU. Rest 2–3 minutes between sets, every set stopped well short of failure. Fall short two sessions running on a new tier: back one tier for a week.
  • Intense split work: PAILs/RAILs at ≤ 70 % effort, 12–20 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position (PAILs/RAILs, effort capped at 70 %). (1) Passive hold, 2 minutes minimum: settle in at the logged support height to a firm stretch and simply breathe, in for four, out for four. This is a warm-in and should feel like letting go, not pushing. Depth will drift a little; let it. (2) PAILs ramp: tense the whole body (fists, trunk, jaw loose), then drive the stretched leg into the floor (front split: front heel and back knee press down; side split: both legs press down and inward), starting at about 10 % effort and ramping steadily over 10–20 seconds to today's ceiling of 70 % of what you could do. Hold the ceiling 5–15 seconds. The range does not change during PAILs; only the tension does. Keep breathing throughout. (3) RAILs, immediately, no release: switch smoothly to contracting the opposing muscles to pull yourself deeper (front split: pull the front heel back toward you and drag the back knee forward through the floor, i.e. hip flexors and hamstring of the back leg working; side split: actively pull the legs apart). 5–15 seconds, same ceiling. (4) Exhale and settle into whatever new depth appeared. That is one round. 2 rounds per position, about 3–5 minutes each including the passive hold. Stop rules. Any tremor that turns into cramp, any sharp sensation, or anything at the knee, inside the hip socket or the sitting-bone tendon ends the position; the 48-hour rule governs. If soreness lasts past 24–36 hours after a PAILs session, the next one runs one notch lower in effort. Progression. Support height moves exactly as in the PNF sessions (hold the first passive 2 minutes with hips square and stretch under 6/10 → next lower support next session). The effort ceiling moves only by the block calendar, never by feel.
  • Back bridge ladder, current tier (last, inside the mobility block). 2–3 sets, 60–90 s rest Tier 1 glute bridge: on your back, knees bent, feet flat, press the hips up until knees-hips-shoulders are a straight line, 2-second squeeze; pass 3 × 12. Tier 2 straight bridge (reverse plank): seated, legs straight, hands on the floor behind, press the hips up until heels-to-shoulders is one line, head neutral, 2-second hold; pass 3 × 12. Tier 3 wall bridge: back to a wall a step away, reach overhead and back, hands on the wall, walk them down a few hand-widths toward hip height and back up, eyes on the hands throughout; pass 3 × 8. Tier 4 incline bridge: hands flat on the bench, fingers pointing toward the feet, feet on the floor, press up into a bridge with hips high and arms as straight as they go, eyes on the hands, 3-second hold; pass 3 × 8. Tier 5 full bridge: hands and feet on the floor, hips high, head off the floor, elbows may bend, 5-second hold; pass 3 × 5. Tier 6 wheel-quality: arms straight, shoulders over the wrists, feet walked in; 3 × 15–20 s. Exhale into every position, four counts in and four out. A pinch at the base of the spine, anything in the neck, or pain at the front of the shoulder or in the wrist ends the set and starts the 48-hour clock. If bench-to-floor is too big a jump, put the hands on a stack of plates and lower it a few centimetres at a time.

Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Rest 90 seconds between legs or 2 minutes between pairs. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then add load. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps.   • Hanging leg raise or knee raise. 3 × 8–15 Dead hang from the pull-up bar, shoulders pulled down. Knee raise: pull the knees up to the chest, curling the pelvis under at the top, lower in two seconds without swinging. Leg raise: legs straight, raise until the feet reach bar height. Stop when the swing starts. Progression: knee raise 8 → 15, then straight-leg at 8.

(2) PAILs ramp: tense the whole body (fists, trunk, jaw loose), then drive the stretched leg into the floor (front split: front heel and back knee press down; side split: both legs press down and inward), starting at about 10 % effort and ramping steadily over 10–20 seconds to today's ceiling of 70 % of what you could do. Hold the ceiling 5–15 seconds. The range does not change during PAILs; only the tension does. Keep breathing throughout. (3) RAILs, immediately, no release: switch smoothly to contracting the opposing muscles to pull yourself deeper (front split: pull the front heel back toward you and drag the back knee forward through the floor, i.e. hip flexors and hamstring of the back leg working; side split: actively pull the legs apart). 5–15 seconds, same ceiling. (4) Exhale and settle into whatever new depth appeared. That is one round. 1–2 rounds per position, about 3–5 minutes each including the passive hold. Stop rules. Any tremor that turns into cramp, any sharp sensation, or anything at the knee, inside the hip socket or the sitting-bone tendon ends the position; the 48-hour rule governs. If soreness lasts past 24–36 hours after a PAILs session, the next one runs one notch lower in effort. Progression. Support height moves exactly as in the PNF sessions (hold the first passive 2 minutes with hips square and stretch under 6/10 → next lower support next session). The effort ceiling moves only by the block calendar, never by feel.

Saturday and Sunday · rest days ≈ 10–15 min daily layer only

  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Nothing else. No pulse-raiser (training days only), no lifting, no intense stretching. Sleep 7–9 hours; a week averaging under 7 means no new tier is tested next week.

Week 10

Jan 25 – 29
loading B

Loading B. If a lift has stalled since week 8, the base document's first fix applies: a fifth set for the squat; hold and keep adding reps for everything else. Equipment check on Tuesday if the bar has arrived.

Monday · Push A ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Bench press (floor press until stands arrive). 4 × 6–12 Floor press: lie on the stall mat, roll the loaded bar over your chest, elbows and upper arms resting on the floor at the bottom. Grip just outside shoulder width, wrists stacked over elbows, shoulder blades pulled down and together, feet flat. Press until the elbows lock, lower under control until the upper arms touch the floor (about two seconds down), pause a beat, press. Every rep from a dead stop on the floor. Stop the set at 1–3 reps shy of failure: the last rep looks like the first, no bouncing the arms off the floor. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Overhead press, cleaned from the floor. 3 × 6–12 Bar on the floor, grip just outside the shoulders, pull it to the collarbone in one movement (the clean). Then: squeeze glutes, brace the trunk, tuck the chin back out of the bar path, press straight up until the elbows lock with the bar over the back of the head and the ears in line with the arms. Lower to the collarbone in two seconds. No leg drive, no lean-back. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; the rep where the low back starts arching is the rep you did not do. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Ring dips (bench dips if rings are not yet clean for 3). 3 × 8–12 Rings at hip height, straps tight. Jump to a support hold: arms locked, rings turned slightly out, shoulders pressed down away from the ears. Lower by bending the elbows, keeping them pointing back, until the shoulder is level with or just below the elbow, two seconds down, press back to lockout. Stop the set the moment the rings start wobbling outward or the shoulders roll forward at the bottom. Rest 2 minutes. Bench dips: hands on the bench edge behind you, feet on the floor, same depth and tempo. Progression on rings is reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Planche-lean primer. 3 × 15–25 s Kneel, place the hands on the floor shoulder-width with the fingers pointing out to the sides or back toward the knees, arms locked. Lift the knees and shift the shoulders forward past the wrists, as far as you can while the elbows stay locked and the ribs stay pulled in (hollow body, no belly hanging). Feet stay on the floor. Hold 15–25 seconds. End the hold while the elbows are still locked and the hips still high: the moment either starts to go, the hold is over. Rest 90 seconds. Progression: hold time 15 → 25 s, then shoulders further forward; this is a primer for Wednesday's ladder, not a hard set. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15–20 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 3 × 12–20 Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Intense split work: static holds + PNF contract-relax, 10–12 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. On this shorter session run 2 cycles per position; a third only if the first two felt easy. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.

Tuesday · Lower + Pull A ≈ 55–60 min knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase. The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Pistol squat ladder, current tier (as the squat's warm-up). 3 sets per leg, alternating, 90 s rest per pair Done straight after the daily layer, in place of the goblet warm-up. Tier 1, bench pistol: stand on one leg in front of the flat bench (about 45 cm), other leg held straight out front, arms reached forward, shoulders rounded forward. Sit back to the bench under control with the heel down and the knee tracking over the middle of the foot, touch, stand without the free foot touching down. Tier 2 is the same to a 25–30 cm target. Tier 3 is full depth (hamstring on calf) with a fingertip on the doorframe or a 5–10 lb plate held out front as a counterweight. Tier 4 is unassisted. Stop every set at the first rep where the heel lifts or the knee dives inward. Pass criteria: tiers 1–2, 3 × 8 clean per leg; tiers 3–4, 3 × 5. Ankle gate first: knee-to-wall under 10 cm keeps the pistol at tier 1 regardless of strength. Wrist prehab sets (below) are done inside these rest periods. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches. Done inside the pistol rest periods.
  • Squat (Zercher or front squat without a rack; Bulgarian split squat until the bar arrives). 4 × 6–12 Zercher: bar on the bench, squat down, hook it in the crooks of the elbows, hands clasped, stand. Feet shoulder-width, toes slightly out. Sit between the heels with the chest up until the thighs are at least parallel, heels down, knees tracking over the toes, then drive up. Two seconds down, one up. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure, or at the rep where the heels lift or the knees cave. Rest 2–3 minutes. If still on split squats: rear foot on the bench, dumbbells in hand, same depth and tempo, 4 × 6–12 per leg. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Romanian deadlift. 3 × 8–12 Bar held at the hips, feet hip-width, knees softly bent and staying that way. Push the hips straight back and let the bar slide down the thighs, back flat, until the hamstrings are fully stretched (usually mid-shin), then drive the hips forward to stand. Three seconds down, one up. The bar never touches the floor between reps. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or when the back begins to round. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Pull-ups, overhand. 3 × 6–12 Dead hang from the doorway bar, hands just outside shoulder-width, palms away. Pull the shoulder blades down first, then the elbows to the ribs, until the chin is clearly over the bar. Lower fully to a straight-arm hang, two seconds. No kip, no swing. Stop at the first rep that does not clear the bar cleanly. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: reps 6 → 12, then a plate in a backpack and back to 6. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Barbell row (ring row, feet elevated, until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Bar on the floor, hinge to about 45°, grip just outside the knees. Pull the bar to the lower ribs, elbows passing the torso, shoulder blades squeezing, pause a beat, lower in two seconds. The torso angle does not change; if it rises to lift the bar, the set is over. Ring row: lie back under the rings with heels on the bench, body a straight line, pull the chest to the rings, lower slowly. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Ball of one foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, other hand on the wall for balance only. Lower the heel to a full stretch, pause one second, rise as high as possible, squeeze one second. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Progression: reps 10 → 20, then heavier dumbbell and back to 10. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Equipment check. If the barbell and plates have landed, move each lift one column left on the base substitution ladder (split squat → Zercher/front squat, ring row → barbell row) and re-find the bottom-of-range weight at 3 reps in reserve. A new variation is a new double-progression run, not a continuation.

Wednesday · Skill ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist · spine (extension)

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs with extra wrist rotations, 6–8 min. Same procedure as every day (one joint at a time, 20–30 % whole-body tension, 15–30 seconds per rotation, neck → shoulders → elbows → wrists → spine → hips → knees → ankles), but the wrists get 5–6 rotations per direction instead of 3, plus a set of slow finger-closed to finger-open circles, because today loads the wrist in extension more than any other day.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Wrist and shoulder prep: wrist push-ups 2 × 10 (fingers forward, then backward), scapular push-ups 2 × 10, hollow-body hold 2 × 20–30 s. Wrist push-ups: on all fours, hands flat, rock the weight forward over the hands until the wrists are at the edge of comfortable extension, then push the palms down so the heels of the hands rise; 10 slow reps, then turn the fingers to point back toward the knees and repeat. Scapular push-ups: plank on locked arms, let the shoulder blades pinch together so the chest drops an inch, then push the floor away until the upper back rounds; elbows never bend. Hollow hold: lie on your back, low back pressed into the mat, arms overhead, legs straight and lifted a few inches, ribs pulled down; hold 20–30 seconds breathing.
  • Planche ladder, current tier. 5–6 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, planche lean: hands on the floor, fingers out or back, arms locked, shoulders pushed forward past the wrists, body hollow, feet on the floor; hold stopped while the elbows are still locked and hips still high. Pass: 3–4 sets of 30 s clean with the shoulders well past the wrists. Tier 2, tuck planche: from the lean, knees tucked to the chest, feet lifted off the floor, hips level with the shoulders. Pass: 30 s clean. Tier 3 advanced tuck (back flat, hips opened to about 90°), tier 4 straddle (legs straight and wide, pass 15–20 s), tier 5 full. Every set ends at the first sign of form loss: hips dropping, elbows bending, shoulders drifting back. 5–6 sets with 2–3 minutes' rest; this is practice, not a workout. Fall short of a new tier two sessions running: back one tier for a week, retry. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier. 4–5 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, pike push-up: hands and feet on the floor, hips pushed high so the torso points at the floor, lower the top of the head to the floor between the hands, press back to locked arms. Pass: 3–4 sets of 10 clean. Tier 2, elevated pike: feet on the bench, torso near vertical, same movement. Pass: 3–4 × 10. Tier 3, wall negatives: kick up to a wall handstand, lower over three full seconds until the head touches, kick down, reset. Pass: 5 × 3 with a 3-second lower. Tier 4, partial-range wall press: stacked books or a block under the head set the depth; press out of the bottom quarter, then half, then three-quarters, 3 × 5 at each depth before shrinking the stack. Tier 5: 3 or more strict full wall HSPU. Rest 2–3 minutes between sets, every set stopped well short of failure. Fall short two sessions running on a new tier: back one tier for a week.
  • Intense split work: PAILs/RAILs at ≤ 70 % effort, 12–20 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position (PAILs/RAILs, effort capped at 70 %). (1) Passive hold, 2 minutes minimum: settle in at the logged support height to a firm stretch and simply breathe, in for four, out for four. This is a warm-in and should feel like letting go, not pushing. Depth will drift a little; let it. (2) PAILs ramp: tense the whole body (fists, trunk, jaw loose), then drive the stretched leg into the floor (front split: front heel and back knee press down; side split: both legs press down and inward), starting at about 10 % effort and ramping steadily over 10–20 seconds to today's ceiling of 70 % of what you could do. Hold the ceiling 5–15 seconds. The range does not change during PAILs; only the tension does. Keep breathing throughout. (3) RAILs, immediately, no release: switch smoothly to contracting the opposing muscles to pull yourself deeper (front split: pull the front heel back toward you and drag the back knee forward through the floor, i.e. hip flexors and hamstring of the back leg working; side split: actively pull the legs apart). 5–15 seconds, same ceiling. (4) Exhale and settle into whatever new depth appeared. That is one round. 2 rounds per position, about 3–5 minutes each including the passive hold. Stop rules. Any tremor that turns into cramp, any sharp sensation, or anything at the knee, inside the hip socket or the sitting-bone tendon ends the position; the 48-hour rule governs. If soreness lasts past 24–36 hours after a PAILs session, the next one runs one notch lower in effort. Progression. Support height moves exactly as in the PNF sessions (hold the first passive 2 minutes with hips square and stretch under 6/10 → next lower support next session). The effort ceiling moves only by the block calendar, never by feel.
  • Back bridge ladder, current tier (last, inside the mobility block). 2–3 sets, 60–90 s rest Tier 1 glute bridge: on your back, knees bent, feet flat, press the hips up until knees-hips-shoulders are a straight line, 2-second squeeze; pass 3 × 12. Tier 2 straight bridge (reverse plank): seated, legs straight, hands on the floor behind, press the hips up until heels-to-shoulders is one line, head neutral, 2-second hold; pass 3 × 12. Tier 3 wall bridge: back to a wall a step away, reach overhead and back, hands on the wall, walk them down a few hand-widths toward hip height and back up, eyes on the hands throughout; pass 3 × 8. Tier 4 incline bridge: hands flat on the bench, fingers pointing toward the feet, feet on the floor, press up into a bridge with hips high and arms as straight as they go, eyes on the hands, 3-second hold; pass 3 × 8. Tier 5 full bridge: hands and feet on the floor, hips high, head off the floor, elbows may bend, 5-second hold; pass 3 × 5. Tier 6 wheel-quality: arms straight, shoulders over the wrists, feet walked in; 3 × 15–20 s. Exhale into every position, four counts in and four out. A pinch at the base of the spine, anything in the neck, or pain at the front of the shoulder or in the wrist ends the set and starts the 48-hour clock. If bench-to-floor is too big a jump, put the hands on a stack of plates and lower it a few centimetres at a time.

The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Deadlift, from the floor. 3 × 6–10 Bar over the middle of the feet, feet hip-width. Hinge down, grip just outside the shins, shins touching the bar, chest up, back flat, arms straight. Push the floor away: hips and shoulders rise together, bar dragging up the shins and thighs until you are standing tall. Lower by pushing the hips back and bending the knees once the bar passes them, two seconds down, bar touching the floor and resetting between reps (no bouncing). Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or at the first rep where the lower back rounds. Rest 3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Bulgarian split squat. 4 × 8–12 per leg Rear foot laced on the bench, front foot far enough forward that the front shin stays roughly vertical at the bottom, dumbbells or plates hanging at the sides. Lower straight down until the rear knee nearly touches the floor and the front thigh is at least parallel, two seconds, drive up through the front heel. Front knee tracks over the middle toes. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Rest 90 seconds between legs or 2 minutes between pairs. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then add load. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Chin-ups, underhand. 3 × 6–12 Dead hang, palms facing you, hands shoulder-width. Shoulder blades down first, then pull until the chin is clearly over the bar, lower fully in two seconds. No kip. Stop at the first rep that does not clear. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: 6 → 12 reps, then backpack load, back to 6. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Barbell row or chest-supported row on the bench (ring row until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Chest-supported: lie face-down on the bench set flat or on a slight incline, dumbbells or bar hanging below, pull to the lower ribs with the elbows passing the torso, squeeze the shoulder blades one second, lower in two seconds. Barbell row as Tuesday. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; momentum or a shifting torso ends the set. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Exactly as Tuesday: ball of the foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, full stretch at the bottom with a one-second pause, full rise with a one-second squeeze, two seconds down. 1–3 reps shy of failure. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Hanging leg raise or knee raise. 3 × 8–15 Dead hang from the pull-up bar, shoulders pulled down. Knee raise: pull the knees up to the chest, curling the pelvis under at the top, lower in two seconds without swinging. Leg raise: legs straight, raise until the feet reach bar height. Stop when the swing starts. Progression: knee raise 8 → 15, then straight-leg at 8.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches.

Friday · Push B ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Close-grip bench press (close-grip floor press until stands arrive). 3 × 8–12 As Monday's floor press but with the hands shoulder-width or a thumb's length inside it, elbows tucked closer to the ribs on the way down so the upper arms touch the floor nearer the body. Dead stop on the floor each rep, two seconds down, press to lockout. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier, as the day's vertical push. 4 × tier reps Same tier and same execution as Wednesday (pike: head to floor between the hands and press to lockout; elevated pike: feet on the bench; wall negatives: three-second lower to head touch; partial press: stacked books set the depth). Four sets, each stopped well short of failure, 2–3 minutes' rest. If Wednesday left any shoulder or wrist tenderness, do pike push-ups one tier down instead; the second weekly exposure is for practice volume, not for testing. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Ring push-ups, feet elevated or plate on the back. 3 × 8–12 Rings a few inches off the floor, straps tight, feet on the bench. Plank on locked arms with the rings turned slightly out, lower until the chest is level with the rings with the elbows pointing back at about 45°, two seconds, press back to lockout and turn the rings out again. Body stays a straight line; the set ends when the hips sag or the rings drift. Rest 2 minutes. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Dumbbell lateral raise. 3 × 12–20 Dumbbells at the sides, slight forward lean, elbows soft. Raise out to the side leading with the elbows until the arms are parallel to the floor, pinkies level with or slightly above the thumbs, one second up, two seconds down. No shrug, no swing from the hips. 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: 12 → 20 reps, then the next dumbbell setting at 12. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15–20 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 3 × 12–20 Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Intense split work: PAILs/RAILs at ≤ 70 % effort, 10–12 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position (PAILs/RAILs, effort capped at 70 %). (1) Passive hold, 2 minutes minimum: settle in at the logged support height to a firm stretch and simply breathe, in for four, out for four. This is a warm-in and should feel like letting go, not pushing. Depth will drift a little; let it. (2) PAILs ramp: tense the whole body (fists, trunk, jaw loose), then drive the stretched leg into the floor (front split: front heel and back knee press down; side split: both legs press down and inward), starting at about 10 % effort and ramping steadily over 10–20 seconds to today's ceiling of 70 % of what you could do. Hold the ceiling 5–15 seconds. The range does not change during PAILs; only the tension does. Keep breathing throughout. (3) RAILs, immediately, no release: switch smoothly to contracting the opposing muscles to pull yourself deeper (front split: pull the front heel back toward you and drag the back knee forward through the floor, i.e. hip flexors and hamstring of the back leg working; side split: actively pull the legs apart). 5–15 seconds, same ceiling. (4) Exhale and settle into whatever new depth appeared. That is one round. 1–2 rounds per position, about 3–5 minutes each including the passive hold. Stop rules. Any tremor that turns into cramp, any sharp sensation, or anything at the knee, inside the hip socket or the sitting-bone tendon ends the position; the 48-hour rule governs. If soreness lasts past 24–36 hours after a PAILs session, the next one runs one notch lower in effort. Progression. Support height moves exactly as in the PNF sessions (hold the first passive 2 minutes with hips square and stretch under 6/10 → next lower support next session). The effort ceiling moves only by the block calendar, never by feel.

Saturday and Sunday · rest days ≈ 10–15 min daily layer only

  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Nothing else. No pulse-raiser (training days only), no lifting, no intense stretching. Sleep 7–9 hours; a week averaging under 7 means no new tier is tested next week.

Week 11

Feb 1 – 5
loading B · last full week

Last full loading week. Nothing special; the retest is a record of the block, not an event to peak for. Write down every load and rep: Block 2 week 1 starts from them.

Monday · Push A ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.

Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Bench press (floor press until stands arrive). 4 × 6–12 Floor press: lie on the stall mat, roll the loaded bar over your chest, elbows and upper arms resting on the floor at the bottom. Grip just outside shoulder width, wrists stacked over elbows, shoulder blades pulled down and together, feet flat. Press until the elbows lock, lower under control until the upper arms touch the floor (about two seconds down), pause a beat, press. Every rep from a dead stop on the floor. Stop the set at 1–3 reps shy of failure: the last rep looks like the first, no bouncing the arms off the floor. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Overhead press, cleaned from the floor. 3 × 6–12 Bar on the floor, grip just outside the shoulders, pull it to the collarbone in one movement (the clean). Then: squeeze glutes, brace the trunk, tuck the chin back out of the bar path, press straight up until the elbows lock with the bar over the back of the head and the ears in line with the arms. Lower to the collarbone in two seconds. No leg drive, no lean-back. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; the rep where the low back starts arching is the rep you did not do. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Ring dips (bench dips if rings are not yet clean for 3). 3 × 8–12 Rings at hip height, straps tight. Jump to a support hold: arms locked, rings turned slightly out, shoulders pressed down away from the ears. Lower by bending the elbows, keeping them pointing back, until the shoulder is level with or just below the elbow, two seconds down, press back to lockout. Stop the set the moment the rings start wobbling outward or the shoulders roll forward at the bottom. Rest 2 minutes. Bench dips: hands on the bench edge behind you, feet on the floor, same depth and tempo. Progression on rings is reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Planche-lean primer. 3 × 15–25 s Kneel, place the hands on the floor shoulder-width with the fingers pointing out to the sides or back toward the knees, arms locked. Lift the knees and shift the shoulders forward past the wrists, as far as you can while the elbows stay locked and the ribs stay pulled in (hollow body, no belly hanging). Feet stay on the floor. Hold 15–25 seconds. End the hold while the elbows are still locked and the hips still high: the moment either starts to go, the hold is over. Rest 90 seconds. Progression: hold time 15 → 25 s, then shoulders further forward; this is a primer for Wednesday's ladder, not a hard set. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15–20 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 3 × 12–20 Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Intense split work: static holds + PNF contract-relax, 10–12 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position. (1) Settle in at the logged support height to the point of a firm but tolerable stretch in the muscle belly, about a 6–7 out of 10: front split, back knee down then straightened, front heel sliding forward, hips square, hands on the support; side split, feet wide, kneecaps pointing up, hands on the floor or bench in front. Hold 30–60 seconds, breathing in for four and out for four. (2) Contract: without moving, press the stretched leg hard into the floor for 5–10 seconds (front split: front heel drags back toward you and the back knee presses down; side split: both legs press down and squeeze inward as if closing the split). Nothing actually moves; you are contracting the muscles being stretched against the floor. Build to a hard but not maximal effort, about 7 out of 10, and keep breathing. (3) Relax fully on a long exhale. In the 5–10 seconds after letting go the stretch eases; sink slightly deeper in that window, a centimetre or two, never a lunge. (4) Hold the new depth 30–60 seconds. That is one cycle. Repeat steps 2–4 for 2–4 cycles, then come out slowly and move to the next position. On this shorter session run 2 cycles per position; a third only if the first two felt easy. Stop rules. Stretch belongs in the back of the thigh, the front of the hip, the inner thigh. Any pain at the knee, deep inside the hip socket, or at the sitting-bone tendon is a 48-hour event: that position is out for the week. Progression. Only the support height moves: when a position can be held at the current height for the full first hold with the hips square and the stretch under 6/10, take the next lower support (blocks → fingers → none) on the next session.
  • Write down. Today's exact loads and reps. Block 2 week 1 starts from them.

  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase.   • Pistol squat ladder, current tier (as the squat's warm-up). 3 sets per leg, alternating, 90 s rest per pair Done straight after the daily layer, in place of the goblet warm-up. Tier 3 is full depth (hamstring on calf) with a fingertip on the doorframe or a 5–10 lb plate held out front as a counterweight. Tier 4 is unassisted.   • Romanian deadlift. 3 × 8–12 Bar held at the hips, feet hip-width, knees softly bent and staying that way. Push the hips straight back and let the bar slide down the thighs, back flat, until the hamstrings are fully stretched (usually mid-shin), then drive the hips forward to stand. Three seconds down, one up. The bar never touches the floor between reps. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or when the back begins to round. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Ring row: lie back under the rings with heels on the bench, body a straight line, pull the chest to the rings, lower slowly. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Wednesday · Skill ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist · spine (extension)

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows.
