This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs with extra wrist rotations, 6–8 min. Same procedure as every day (one joint at a time, 20–30 % whole-body tension, 15–30 seconds per rotation, neck → shoulders → elbows → wrists → spine → hips → knees → ankles), but the wrists get 5–6 rotations per direction instead of 3, plus a set of slow finger-closed to finger-open circles, because today loads the wrist in extension more than any other day.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Wrist and shoulder prep: wrist push-ups 2 × 10 (fingers forward, then backward), scapular push-ups 2 × 10, hollow-body hold 2 × 20–30 s. Wrist push-ups: on all fours, hands flat, rock the weight forward over the hands until the wrists are at the edge of comfortable extension, then push the palms down so the heels of the hands rise; 10 slow reps, then turn the fingers to point back toward the knees and repeat. Scapular push-ups: plank on locked arms, let the shoulder blades pinch together so the chest drops an inch, then push the floor away until the upper back rounds; elbows never bend. Hollow hold: lie on your back, low back pressed into the mat, arms overhead, legs straight and lifted a few inches, ribs pulled down; hold 20–30 seconds breathing.
  • Planche ladder, current tier. 5–6 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, planche lean: hands on the floor, fingers out or back, arms locked, shoulders pushed forward past the wrists, body hollow, feet on the floor; hold stopped while the elbows are still locked and hips still high. Pass: 3–4 sets of 30 s clean with the shoulders well past the wrists. Tier 2, tuck planche: from the lean, knees tucked to the chest, feet lifted off the floor, hips level with the shoulders. Pass: 30 s clean. Tier 3 advanced tuck (back flat, hips opened to about 90°), tier 4 straddle (legs straight and wide, pass 15–20 s), tier 5 full. Every set ends at the first sign of form loss: hips dropping, elbows bending, shoulders drifting back. 5–6 sets with 2–3 minutes' rest; this is practice, not a workout. Fall short of a new tier two sessions running: back one tier for a week, retry. Tier rule: advance only after 3–4 sets of 8–12 clean reps or a clean 30 s hold at the current tier; two straight sessions short on a new tier means back one tier for a week. No new tier is tested in a week averaging under 7 hours' sleep.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier. 4–5 sets, 2–3 min rest Tier 1, pike push-up: hands and feet on the floor, hips pushed high so the torso points at the floor, lower the top of the head to the floor between the hands, press back to locked arms. Pass: 3–4 sets of 10 clean. Tier 2, elevated pike: feet on the bench, torso near vertical, same movement. Pass: 3–4 × 10. Tier 3, wall negatives: kick up to a wall handstand, lower over three full seconds until the head touches, kick down, reset. Pass: 5 × 3 with a 3-second lower. Tier 4, partial-range wall press: stacked books or a block under the head set the depth; press out of the bottom quarter, then half, then three-quarters, 3 × 5 at each depth before shrinking the stack. Tier 5: 3 or more strict full wall HSPU. Rest 2–3 minutes between sets, every set stopped well short of failure. Fall short two sessions running on a new tier: back one tier for a week.
  • Intense split work: PAILs/RAILs at ≤ 70 % effort, 12–20 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). For the side split also note hip height from the floor in hand-widths. This is not an assumption about your flexibility; it is the measurement. Whatever height lets you hold the position with square hips and no joint pain is today's starting point. Procedure per position (PAILs/RAILs, effort capped at 70 %). (1) Passive hold, 2 minutes minimum: settle in at the logged support height to a firm stretch and simply breathe, in for four, out for four. This is a warm-in and should feel like letting go, not pushing. Depth will drift a little; let it. (2) PAILs ramp: tense the whole body (fists, trunk, jaw loose), then drive the stretched leg into the floor (front split: front heel and back knee press down; side split: both legs press down and inward), starting at about 10 % effort and ramping steadily over 10–20 seconds to today's ceiling of 70 % of what you could do. Hold the ceiling 5–15 seconds. The range does not change during PAILs; only the tension does. Keep breathing throughout. (3) RAILs, immediately, no release: switch smoothly to contracting the opposing muscles to pull yourself deeper (front split: pull the front heel back toward you and drag the back knee forward through the floor, i.e. hip flexors and hamstring of the back leg working; side split: actively pull the legs apart). 5–15 seconds, same ceiling. (4) Exhale and settle into whatever new depth appeared. That is one round. 2 rounds per position, about 3–5 minutes each including the passive hold. Stop rules. Any tremor that turns into cramp, any sharp sensation, or anything at the knee, inside the hip socket or the sitting-bone tendon ends the position; the 48-hour rule governs. If soreness lasts past 24–36 hours after a PAILs session, the next one runs one notch lower in effort. Progression. Support height moves exactly as in the PNF sessions (hold the first passive 2 minutes with hips square and stretch under 6/10 → next lower support next session). The effort ceiling moves only by the block calendar, never by feel.
  • Back bridge ladder, current tier (last, inside the mobility block). 2–3 sets, 60–90 s rest Tier 1 glute bridge: on your back, knees bent, feet flat, press the hips up until knees-hips-shoulders are a straight line, 2-second squeeze; pass 3 × 12. Tier 2 straight bridge (reverse plank): seated, legs straight, hands on the floor behind, press the hips up until heels-to-shoulders is one line, head neutral, 2-second hold; pass 3 × 12. Tier 3 wall bridge: back to a wall a step away, reach overhead and back, hands on the wall, walk them down a few hand-widths toward hip height and back up, eyes on the hands throughout; pass 3 × 8. Tier 4 incline bridge: hands flat on the bench, fingers pointing toward the feet, feet on the floor, press up into a bridge with hips high and arms as straight as they go, eyes on the hands, 3-second hold; pass 3 × 8. Tier 5 full bridge: hands and feet on the floor, hips high, head off the floor, elbows may bend, 5-second hold; pass 3 × 5. Tier 6 wheel-quality: arms straight, shoulders over the wrists, feet walked in; 3 × 15–20 s. Exhale into every position, four counts in and four out. A pinch at the base of the spine, anything in the neck, or pain at the front of the shoulder or in the wrist ends the set and starts the 48-hour clock. If bench-to-floor is too big a jump, put the hands on a stack of plates and lower it a few centimetres at a time.

Thursday · Lower + Pull B ≈ 55–60 min knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase. The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Deadlift, from the floor. 3 × 6–10 Bar over the middle of the feet, feet hip-width. Hinge down, grip just outside the shins, shins touching the bar, chest up, back flat, arms straight. Push the floor away: hips and shoulders rise together, bar dragging up the shins and thighs until you are standing tall. Lower by pushing the hips back and bending the knees once the bar passes them, two seconds down, bar touching the floor and resetting between reps (no bouncing). Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or at the first rep where the lower back rounds. Rest 3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Bulgarian split squat. 4 × 8–12 per leg Rear foot laced on the bench, front foot far enough forward that the front shin stays roughly vertical at the bottom, dumbbells or plates hanging at the sides. Lower straight down until the rear knee nearly touches the floor and the front thigh is at least parallel, two seconds, drive up through the front heel. Front knee tracks over the middle toes. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Rest 90 seconds between legs or 2 minutes between pairs. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then add load. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Chin-ups, underhand. 3 × 6–12 Dead hang, palms facing you, hands shoulder-width. Shoulder blades down first, then pull until the chin is clearly over the bar, lower fully in two seconds. No kip. Stop at the first rep that does not clear. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: 6 → 12 reps, then backpack load, back to 6. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Barbell row or chest-supported row on the bench (ring row until the bar arrives). 3 × 8–12 Chest-supported: lie face-down on the bench set flat or on a slight incline, dumbbells or bar hanging below, pull to the lower ribs with the elbows passing the torso, squeeze the shoulder blades one second, lower in two seconds. Barbell row as Tuesday. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; momentum or a shifting torso ends the set. Rest 2 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 4 × 10–20 Exactly as Tuesday: ball of the foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, full stretch at the bottom with a one-second pause, full rise with a one-second squeeze, two seconds down. 1–3 reps shy of failure. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Hanging leg raise or knee raise. 3 × 8–15 Dead hang from the pull-up bar, shoulders pulled down. Knee raise: pull the knees up to the chest, curling the pelvis under at the top, lower in two seconds without swinging. Leg raise: legs straight, raise until the feet reach bar height. Stop when the swing starts. Progression: knee raise 8 → 15, then straight-leg at 8.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches.
  • Write down. Today's exact loads and reps on every line.

Dead stop on the floor each rep, two seconds down, press to lockout. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Rest 2–3 minutes. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier, as the day's vertical push. 4 × tier reps Same tier and same execution as Wednesday (pike: head to floor between the hands and press to lockout; elevated pike: feet on the bench; wall negatives: three-second lower to head touch; partial press: stacked books set the depth). Four sets, each stopped well short of failure, 2–3 minutes' rest.   • Ring push-ups, feet elevated or plate on the back. 3 × 8–12 Rings a few inches off the floor, straps tight, feet on the bench. Plank on locked arms with the rings turned slightly out, lower until the chest is level with the rings with the elbows pointing back at about 45°, two seconds, press back to lockout and turn the rings out again. Body stays a straight line; the set ends when the hips sag or the rings drift. Rest 2 minutes. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Dumbbell lateral raise. 3 × 12–20 Dumbbells at the sides, slight forward lean, elbows soft. Raise out to the side leading with the elbows until the arms are parallel to the floor, pinkies level with or slightly above the thumbs, one second up, two seconds down. No shrug, no swing from the hips. 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: 12 → 20 reps, then the next dumbbell setting at 12. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15–20 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 3 × 12–20 Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. This week: double progression continues from last session's reps. Add a rep somewhere each session; smallest increment and back to the bottom of the range when every set hits the top. 1–3 reps shy of failure, last rep looks like the first.
  • Intense split work: PAILs/RAILs at ≤ 70 % effort, 10–12 min. Positions, in this order every session: front split left leg forward, front split right leg forward, side split. Before the first hold, write today's hand-support height in the log for each position: blocks (hands on yoga blocks or two stacked books), fingers (fingertips on the floor), or none (hands off the floor, or hamstring/inner thigh resting down). Week 12

Feb 8 – 12
retesttape · month 3

Retest week at about 60 % of normal set volume so every measurement is taken fresh. One top set per main lift to 1 rep in reserve, best clean attempt per ladder, both baseline batteries repeated on Wednesday, splits support height measured not stretched, full tape Friday morning. Then stop and read the numbers.

Monday · Push A ≈ 40 min (retest volume) shoulder · wrist

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Bench press (floor press until stands arrive). 1 top set to 1 rep in reserve, after 2 ramp sets Floor press: lie on the stall mat, roll the loaded bar over your chest, elbows and upper arms resting on the floor at the bottom. Grip just outside shoulder width, wrists stacked over elbows, shoulder blades pulled down and together, feet flat. Press until the elbows lock, lower under control until the upper arms touch the floor (about two seconds down), pause a beat, press. Every rep from a dead stop on the floor. Stop the set at 1–3 reps shy of failure: the last rep looks like the first, no bouncing the arms off the floor. Rest 2–3 minutes. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Overhead press, cleaned from the floor. 1 top set to 1 rep in reserve Bar on the floor, grip just outside the shoulders, pull it to the collarbone in one movement (the clean). Then: squeeze glutes, brace the trunk, tuck the chin back out of the bar path, press straight up until the elbows lock with the bar over the back of the head and the ears in line with the arms. Lower to the collarbone in two seconds. No leg drive, no lean-back. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; the rep where the low back starts arching is the rep you did not do. Rest 2–3 minutes. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Ring dips (bench dips if rings are not yet clean for 3). 2 sets Rings at hip height, straps tight. Jump to a support hold: arms locked, rings turned slightly out, shoulders pressed down away from the ears. Lower by bending the elbows, keeping them pointing back, until the shoulder is level with or just below the elbow, two seconds down, press back to lockout. Stop the set the moment the rings start wobbling outward or the shoulders roll forward at the bottom. Rest 2 minutes. Bench dips: hands on the bench edge behind you, feet on the floor, same depth and tempo. Progression on rings is reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Planche-lean primer. 2 holds Kneel, place the hands on the floor shoulder-width with the fingers pointing out to the sides or back toward the knees, arms locked. Lift the knees and shift the shoulders forward past the wrists, as far as you can while the elbows stay locked and the ribs stay pulled in (hollow body, no belly hanging). Feet stay on the floor. Hold 15–25 seconds. End the hold while the elbows are still locked and the hips still high: the moment either starts to go, the hold is over. Rest 90 seconds. Progression: hold time 15 → 25 s, then shoulders further forward; this is a primer for Wednesday's ladder, not a hard set. Retest: current tier, reduced sets; the best clean attempt is logged on Wednesday.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 2 sets Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.

Front split each leg and side split, exactly the daily-touch procedure: hands at the logged support height, slide into a 4-out-of-10 stretch, hold 20–30 seconds breathing four in and four out, come up, 2–4 reps per position. No contract phase, no PAILs, no sinking on the exhale. The daily touch on its own clears the Thomas et al. weekly threshold, so nothing is lost this week.

Tuesday · Lower + Pull A ≈ 40 min (retest volume) knee · hip · spine

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs, 5–7 min, one joint at a time. Stand or sit tall and brace lightly (about 20–30 % whole-body tension: trunk tight, loose fists, breathing normally). Isolate one joint; everything else stays still. Draw the largest smooth circle that joint can make today, taking 15–30 seconds per full rotation, stopping just short of any pinch and going around it. 3 rotations each direction per joint (5 on a slow day), both sides where paired. Order every time: neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, spine (segment by segment, cat-to-cow then side-bend circles), hips (standing, one knee lifted, knee drawn across then out and back), knees (seated, foot drawing circles with the shin), ankles. Skip no joint; if one catches, make its circle smaller, not faster.
  • Gentle front-split touch, both legs, 3–5 min total. Kneel in a lunge, back knee down on the mat, hands on the floor or on the blocks at the support height you logged (blocks / fingers / none). Slide the front heel forward until you feel a mild stretch in the back of the front thigh and the front of the rear hip, a stretch you could hold while talking normally, roughly a 4 out of 10. Keep the hips square (both hip bones facing straight ahead) and breathe in for four, out for four. Hold 20–30 seconds, come up, repeat 2–3 times, then switch legs. No contracting, no pushing deeper on the exhale: this is the daily dose, not the session. Per Thomas et al. 2018 it is the daily accumulation that drives range, and per Bandy & Irion 1997 and Page 2012, 20–30 seconds per rep and 2–4 reps per position is the whole effective dose.
  • Gentle side-split touch, 3–5 min total. Stand with feet wide, toes pointing forward or slightly out, hands on the floor in front of you (or on the bench/blocks if the floor is out of reach). Walk the feet apart until the inner thighs register a mild 4-out-of-10 stretch, knees straight but not locked hard, kneecaps pointing up, not forward. Hold 20–30 seconds breathing in for four and out for four, walk the feet in to rest, repeat 2–4 times. If you feel anything at the inside of the knee or deep in the hip socket rather than along the inner thigh, narrow the stance until you don't.
  • Why gentle today. The two touch lines above are the whole of today's split work: 20–30 s holds, 2–4 reps per position, a 4-out-of-10 stretch, no contract phase. The legs are about to be squatted or hinged, so this is a lower day, not an intense splits day; the three intense sessions are Monday, Wednesday and Friday. The touch can be done before the pulse-raiser or after the last calf set, whichever is easier to keep daily.
  • Pistol squat ladder, current tier (as the squat's warm-up). 1 best clean set per leg at the current tier, before the squat; log tier and reps Done straight after the daily layer, in place of the goblet warm-up. Tier 1, bench pistol: stand on one leg in front of the flat bench (about 45 cm), other leg held straight out front, arms reached forward, shoulders rounded forward. Sit back to the bench under control with the heel down and the knee tracking over the middle of the foot, touch, stand without the free foot touching down. Tier 2 is the same to a 25–30 cm target. Tier 3 is full depth (hamstring on calf) with a fingertip on the doorframe or a 5–10 lb plate held out front as a counterweight. Tier 4 is unassisted. Stop every set at the first rep where the heel lifts or the knee dives inward. Pass criteria: tiers 1–2, 3 × 8 clean per leg; tiers 3–4, 3 × 5. Ankle gate first: knee-to-wall under 10 cm keeps the pistol at tier 1 regardless of strength. Wrist prehab sets (below) are done inside these rest periods.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15–20 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches. Done inside the pistol rest periods.
  • Squat (Zercher or front squat without a rack; Bulgarian split squat until the bar arrives). 2 ramp sets, then 1 top set to 1 rep in reserve Zercher: bar on the bench, squat down, hook it in the crooks of the elbows, hands clasped, stand. Feet shoulder-width, toes slightly out. Sit between the heels with the chest up until the thighs are at least parallel, heels down, knees tracking over the toes, then drive up. Two seconds down, one up. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure, or at the rep where the heels lift or the knees cave. Rest 2–3 minutes. If still on split squats: rear foot on the bench, dumbbells in hand, same depth and tempo, 4 × 6–12 per leg. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Romanian deadlift. 1 top set to 1 rep in reserve Bar held at the hips, feet hip-width, knees softly bent and staying that way. Push the hips straight back and let the bar slide down the thighs, back flat, until the hamstrings are fully stretched (usually mid-shin), then drive the hips forward to stand. Three seconds down, one up. The bar never touches the floor between reps. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or when the back begins to round. Rest 2 minutes. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Pull-ups, overhand. 1 top set (note backpack load) Dead hang from the doorway bar, hands just outside shoulder-width, palms away. Pull the shoulder blades down first, then the elbows to the ribs, until the chin is clearly over the bar. Lower fully to a straight-arm hang, two seconds. No kip, no swing. Stop at the first rep that does not clear the bar cleanly. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: reps 6 → 12, then a plate in a backpack and back to 6. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Barbell row (ring row, feet elevated, until the bar arrives). 2 sets Bar on the floor, hinge to about 45°, grip just outside the knees. Pull the bar to the lower ribs, elbows passing the torso, shoulder blades squeezing, pause a beat, lower in two seconds. The torso angle does not change; if it rises to lift the bar, the set is over. Ring row: lie back under the rings with heels on the bench, body a straight line, pull the chest to the rings, lower slowly. Rest 2 minutes. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 2 sets Ball of one foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, other hand on the wall for balance only. Lower the heel to a full stretch, pause one second, rise as high as possible, squeeze one second. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Progression: reps 10 → 20, then heavier dumbbell and back to 10. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Record. Load × reps per top set; for pull-ups, backpack load and reps; pistol tier and reps per leg.

Wednesday · Skill ≈ 55–60 min shoulder · wrist · spine (extension)

  • Pulse-raiser, 3 min. March in place, step up and down on the flat bench, shadow-box, or skip rope if the ceiling allows. Continuous and easy: the target is breathing slightly harder and skin feeling warm (roughly 100–120 beats per minute; no monitor needed). Stop at three minutes whether or not it felt like anything. This is not conditioning; it raises tissue temperature before the joints are taken through range.
  • CARs with extra wrist rotations, 6–8 min. Same procedure as every day (one joint at a time, 20–30 % whole-body tension, 15–30 seconds per rotation, neck → shoulders → elbows → wrists → spine → hips → knees → ankles), but the wrists get 5–6 rotations per direction instead of 3, plus a set of slow finger-closed to finger-open circles, because today loads the wrist in extension more than any other day.
  • Cuff wake-up: band external rotation, 2 × 15. Loop the light band on the door anchor at elbow height, elbow pinned to the ribs with a rolled towel between, forearm parallel to the floor. Rotate the hand outward until the forearm points straight ahead of the shoulder, two seconds out, two seconds back, no shoulder shrug. Easy, not a work set.
  • Wrist and shoulder prep: wrist push-ups 2 × 10 (fingers forward, then backward), scapular push-ups 2 × 10, hollow-body hold 2 × 20–30 s. Wrist push-ups: on all fours, hands flat, rock the weight forward over the hands until the wrists are at the edge of comfortable extension, then push the palms down so the heels of the hands rise; 10 slow reps, then turn the fingers to point back toward the knees and repeat. Scapular push-ups: plank on locked arms, let the shoulder blades pinch together so the chest drops an inch, then push the floor away until the upper back rounds; elbows never bend. Hollow hold: lie on your back, low back pressed into the mat, arms overhead, legs straight and lifted a few inches, ribs pulled down; hold 20–30 seconds breathing.
  • Planche ladder, current tier. best clean hold at the current tier, 3 attempts, 3 min rest Tier 1, planche lean: hands on the floor, fingers out or back, arms locked, shoulders pushed forward past the wrists, body hollow, feet on the floor; hold stopped while the elbows are still locked and hips still high. Pass: 3–4 sets of 30 s clean with the shoulders well past the wrists. Tier 2, tuck planche: from the lean, knees tucked to the chest, feet lifted off the floor, hips level with the shoulders. Pass: 30 s clean. Tier 3 advanced tuck (back flat, hips opened to about 90°), tier 4 straddle (legs straight and wide, pass 15–20 s), tier 5 full. Every set ends at the first sign of form loss: hips dropping, elbows bending, shoulders drifting back. 5–6 sets with 2–3 minutes' rest; this is practice, not a workout. Fall short of a new tier two sessions running: back one tier for a week, retry.
  • Handstand push-up ladder, current tier. best clean set at the current tier, 2 attempts Tier 1, pike push-up: hands and feet on the floor, hips pushed high so the torso points at the floor, lower the top of the head to the floor between the hands, press back to locked arms. Pass: 3–4 sets of 10 clean. Tier 2, elevated pike: feet on the bench, torso near vertical, same movement. Pass: 3–4 × 10. Tier 3, wall negatives: kick up to a wall handstand, lower over three full seconds until the head touches, kick down, reset. Pass: 5 × 3 with a 3-second lower. Tier 4, partial-range wall press: stacked books or a block under the head set the depth; press out of the bottom quarter, then half, then three-quarters, 3 × 5 at each depth before shrinking the stack. Tier 5: 3 or more strict full wall HSPU. Rest 2–3 minutes between sets, every set stopped well short of failure. Fall short two sessions running on a new tier: back one tier for a week.
  • Splits measurement, one gentle set per position. Front split left, front split right, side split: slide in with the hands at the lowest support that still allows square hips and a 5–6-out-of-10 stretch, hold 30 seconds, and record that support height (blocks / fingers / none) and, for the side split, hip height from the floor in hand-widths. Compare directly to the week-1 log. No contracting, no pushing: the measurement is support height, not a maximal stretch.
  • Back bridge ladder, current tier (last, inside the mobility block). 1 best clean hold at the current tier after the ladders and before the battery; log tier and seconds Tier 1 glute bridge: on your back, knees bent, feet flat, press the hips up until knees-hips-shoulders are a straight line, 2-second squeeze; pass 3 × 12. Tier 2 straight bridge (reverse plank): seated, legs straight, hands on the floor behind, press the hips up until heels-to-shoulders is one line, head neutral, 2-second hold; pass 3 × 12. Tier 3 wall bridge: back to a wall a step away, reach overhead and back, hands on the wall, walk them down a few hand-widths toward hip height and back up, eyes on the hands throughout; pass 3 × 8. Tier 4 incline bridge: hands flat on the bench, fingers pointing toward the feet, feet on the floor, press up into a bridge with hips high and arms as straight as they go, eyes on the hands, 3-second hold; pass 3 × 8. Tier 5 full bridge: hands and feet on the floor, hips high, head off the floor, elbows may bend, 5-second hold; pass 3 × 5. Tier 6 wheel-quality: arms straight, shoulders over the wrists, feet walked in; 3 × 15–20 s. Exhale into every position, four counts in and four out. A pinch at the base of the spine, anything in the neck, or pain at the front of the shoulder or in the wrist ends the set and starts the 48-hour clock. If bench-to-floor is too big a jump, put the hands on a stack of plates and lower it a few centimetres at a time.
  • Longevity battery + full-body flexibility battery (Sections 05 and 08). same tests, same method as week 1 Run after the ladder attempts and the bridge hold and before the splits measurement: sitting-rising score, one-leg stance each side, standing toe reach, knee-to-wall each side; then Apley's scratch each side, modified Thomas each leg, seated trunk rotation each direction, sit-and-reach. Same tape, same landmarks, same order as week 1. Beighton is not repeated.

  • Deadlift, from the floor. 2 ramp sets, then 1 top set to 1 rep in reserve Bar over the middle of the feet, feet hip-width. Hinge down, grip just outside the shins, shins touching the bar, chest up, back flat, arms straight. Push the floor away: hips and shoulders rise together, bar dragging up the shins and thighs until you are standing tall. Lower by pushing the hips back and bending the knees once the bar passes them, two seconds down, bar touching the floor and resetting between reps (no bouncing). Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure or at the first rep where the lower back rounds. Rest 3 minutes. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Bulgarian split squat. 2 sets per leg Rear foot laced on the bench, front foot far enough forward that the front shin stays roughly vertical at the bottom, dumbbells or plates hanging at the sides. Lower straight down until the rear knee nearly touches the floor and the front thigh is at least parallel, two seconds, drive up through the front heel. Front knee tracks over the middle toes. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure per leg. Rest 90 seconds between legs or 2 minutes between pairs. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then add load. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Chin-ups, underhand. 1 top set (note load) Dead hang, palms facing you, hands shoulder-width. Shoulder blades down first, then pull until the chin is clearly over the bar, lower fully in two seconds. No kip. Stop at the first rep that does not clear. Rest 2–3 minutes. Progression: 6 → 12 reps, then backpack load, back to 6. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Barbell row or chest-supported row on the bench (ring row until the bar arrives). 2 sets Chest-supported: lie face-down on the bench set flat or on a slight incline, dumbbells or bar hanging below, pull to the lower ribs with the elbows passing the torso, squeeze the shoulder blades one second, lower in two seconds. Barbell row as Tuesday. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure; momentum or a shifting torso ends the set. Rest 2 minutes. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Standing calf raise, single-leg. 2 sets Exactly as Tuesday: ball of the foot on a plate edge, dumbbell in the same-side hand, full stretch at the bottom with a one-second pause, full rise with a one-second squeeze, two seconds down. 1–3 reps shy of failure. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Hanging leg raise or knee raise. 2 sets Dead hang from the pull-up bar, shoulders pulled down. Knee raise: pull the knees up to the chest, curling the pelvis under at the top, lower in two seconds without swinging. Leg raise: legs straight, raise until the feet reach bar height. Stop when the swing starts. Progression: knee raise 8 → 15, then straight-leg at 8.
  • Wrist prehab: dumbbell wrist curls + wrist extensions. 2 × 15 each Forearm resting on the thigh or bench, hand hanging off the edge. Curls: palm up, let the dumbbell roll to the fingertips, curl the wrist up fully, two seconds each way. Extensions: palm down, lower the knuckles toward the floor, lift them as high as the wrist goes. 35 lb dumbbell or lighter; it should feel like a pump, never a strain. First thing to drop if a wrist ever aches.

Friday · Push B + tape ≈ 40 min (retest volume) shoulder · wrist

  • Monthly tape, this morning before anything else. 8 measurements + Navy body fat Same tape, same landmarks as the baseline table: shoulder circumference (over both deltoids, under the armpits), chest at the nipple line relaxed, waist at the navel relaxed, neck just below the larynx, flexed upper arm at the peak, flexed forearm at the widest, thigh at the midpoint hip-crease to kneecap, calf at the widest standing. Recompute Navy body fat from neck, waist and height (495 ÷ (1.0324 − 0.19077·log₁₀(waist − neck) + 0.15456·log₁₀(height)) − 450). Third point. Read direction only: waist down, shoulder and chest up, thigh holding, arm/calf/neck closer together; compute arm ÷ 18.0 and calf ÷ 22.5.

Dead stop on the floor each rep, two seconds down, press to lockout. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Rest 2–3 minutes. Body stays a straight line; the set ends when the hips sag or the rings drift. Rest 2 minutes. Progression: reps 8 → 12, then a plate in a backpack.   • Dumbbell lateral raise. 2 sets Dumbbells at the sides, slight forward lean, elbows soft. Raise out to the side leading with the elbows until the arms are parallel to the floor, pinkies level with or slightly above the thumbs, one second up, two seconds down. No shrug, no swing from the hips. 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: 12 → 20 reps, then the next dumbbell setting at 12. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Shoulder prehab: band external rotation at 90° + band face pulls. 2 × 15 each External rotation at 90°: upper arm out to the side parallel to the floor, elbow bent 90°, band anchored low; rotate the forearm from pointing forward to pointing straight up, two seconds each way. Face pulls: band anchored at face height, pull the handles to the ears with the elbows high and wide, pulling the shoulder blades together, pause one second, return slowly. Light enough that 20 reps are smooth; this is tendon conditioning, never to failure.
  • Seated (bent-knee) calf raise. 2 sets Sit on the bench with the balls of the feet on a plate edge or thick book, dumbbell or plate resting across the knees. Lower the heels until you feel a full stretch in the calf, pause one second at the bottom, rise onto the toes as high as you can and squeeze one second at the top. Two seconds down. Stop 1–3 reps shy of failure. Progression: reps 12 → 20, then add load and drop back to 12. Retest: see the set count on this line; the top set is the current working weight taken to 1 rep in reserve, the same stopping point as every other set in the programme. Not a max.
  • Splits, gentle only, 6–10 min. Front split each leg and side split, exactly the daily-touch procedure: hands at the logged support height, slide into a 4-out-of-10 stretch, hold 20–30 seconds breathing four in and four out, come up, 2–4 reps per position. No contract phase, no PAILs, no sinking on the exhale. The daily touch on its own clears the Thomas et al. weekly threshold, so nothing is lost this week.
  • Then stop and read the numbers. Section 04's week-12 columns, Section 05's reading guide, Section 08's flexibility expectations, before writing Block 2 (Section 13).

04Skill pacing over 12 weeks: what to expect, and what a stall looks like

The base document's timelines are the honest ones: planche 1–3 years, three strict wall HSPU 4–8 months, front split 3–12 months, side split 6–18 months; the mobility companion adds pistol squat 3–6 months (6–12 if the ankle gate fails first) and full bridge 2–5 months. Twelve weeks is roughly a quarter of the fastest HSPU timeline and a tenth of the slowest planche one. So the question for this block is not "is the goal reached" but "is the ladder being climbed at a rate consistent with those ranges." The table gives the plausible band; the tier rule, not the table, decides when you actually move. The four original rows and the pistol/bridge rows carry the numbers already established in this page's earlier version and the mobility companion; nothing has been inflated.

LadderWeek 1–2 (likely)Week 6 (plausible)Week 12 (plausible)Would be a stall
Planche
Wed main · Mon primer		Tier 1, planche lean. Working toward 30 s with shoulders well past the wrists.Tier 1 passed or close. First tuck attempts, feet barely off the floor for a few seconds.Tier 2, tuck planche, holds in the 5–15 s range. A clean 30 s tuck by week 12 would be fast, not expected.Lean still under 20 s clean at week 12, with no wrist or shoulder ache to explain it. First fix: check the lean is really a lean (shoulders forward) and not an arm-strength hold.
Handstand push-up
Wed · Fri		Tier 1, pike push-up, or tier 2 if the placement test passes.Tier 2, elevated pike push-up, building to 3–4 × 10.Tier 2 passed; tier 3 wall negatives started, 5 × 3 with a 3-second lower. Reaching tier 4 partial presses by week 12 would be the fast end of the 4–8-month range.Still on tier 1 at week 12. First fix: check Wednesday's HSPU sets are really done fresh with the full 2–3 minutes' rest, and use the Friday fallback (pike one tier down) whenever Wednesday left tenderness, rather than grinding the tier twice a week.
Pistol squat
Tue		Week 2 placement. Ankle gate read from the week-1 knee-to-wall. Tier 1 (bench pistol) tested once; a returning lifter with split squats in the programme very often passes it first try.Tier 2 passed or close; first assisted full-depth reps (tier 3) with a counterweight or a fingertip on the doorframe.Tier 3 passed on at least one side; first unassisted full pistols on the stronger side are the fast-but-plausible end. Both sides clean by week 12 would be ahead of the range, not expected.Still on tier 1 at week 12 with the ankle gate passing and no knee or ankle ache. First fix: confirm the free leg is held out front (not tucked) and the arms are reaching forward; most "strength" stalls are balance stalls.

Shoulders are usually the limiter, not the spine.Tier 5 (full bridge, elbows bent) reached on at least one attempt; 3 × 5 with a 5-second hold by week 12 is the fast end. Wheel-quality (tier 6) is not expected.Still at tier 3 at week 12 with no shoulder or wrist ache. First fix: lower the incline surface a few centimetres at a time instead of jumping bench-to-floor, and check the hands are flat with fingers toward the feet.

A floor-flat front split at week 12 is possible only if week 1 was already near the floor.No change in support height on either side by week 12. Small is normal here.Measurable but modest drop in hip height. The 6–18-month range means week 12 is early.Any inside-hip or knee pain at any point is a 48-hour event, not a stall. A stall with no pain is just the side split being the side split.

The flexibility baseline tests: what week 12 can honestly be expected to show

These five tests (Section 08) are new to this version, so unlike the ladders there is no earlier number on this page to carry forward, and the literature does not give one either. What it does give is a direction and a rough size, and that is what is written below. Where no number is supportable, the table says so, in the same spirit as the "still open" sections elsewhere in this programme.

TestWeek 12, plausibleWould be a stall or a warningWhat the evidence does and does not support
Sit-and-reachReach increases by a few centimetres, most likely in the 2–5 cm range, more if week 1 was well short of the toes. Direction up is the expectation; the size is a soft estimate.No change or a decrease with a clean log. First check: was the daily touch actually daily? Second: is the front-split work finding the hamstring (stretch in the back of the thigh) or the knee?Static and PNF stretching reliably improve sit-and-reach over 4–12 weeks (Thomas 2018; Ingram 2024; Bandy & Irion 1997 used knee-extension ROM, not sit-and-reach). People with poor baseline gain more (Ingram). A precise cm figure for you is not something the literature can give; the 2–5 cm band is a reading of typical trial effect sizes, not a promise.
Apley's scratchGap between fingertips closes by about a finger-width to a hand-width on the tighter side; the better side may already overlap and not move. Asymmetry narrowing is the most meaningful read.Gap widening on either side, or new pain at the front of the shoulder during the test. Either is a joint-health signal worth more than any lift number.Nothing in this programme targets the test directly except the back bridge (shoulder extension and overhead range), the overhead press and CARs, so a modest change is plausible, not predicted. There is no trial dosing shoulder stretching the way Thomas and Ingram dose the lower body; honestly, no number is supportable here beyond "direction."
Modified ThomasThe hanging thigh sits closer to level, or the knee bends closer to 90°, on the side that was tighter. If it was already level with a 90° knee, unchanged is the correct result.Hanging thigh rising further off level, or the knee straightening more, with a clean log. First check: is the back leg's hip actually opening in the front split (rear knee down, hips square), or is the low back arching to fake the range?The front split loads exactly what this test measures (rear-leg hip flexor length). Improvement is the expected direction; the test is scored by eye at home and is not precise enough to put a degree figure on. Treated as pass/level/not-level, not as a number.
